</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In US$ million, unless noted otherwise. EBITDA = Operating income + Depreciation of PP&amp;E + Depreciation of the right of
use assets + Amortization of intangible assets + Unproductive exploratory drillings + (Reversal) / Deterioration of PP&amp;E. Adjusted EBITDA = EBITDA that excludes IFRS 16 effects +/- <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">one-off</FONT> items. Net
Leverage Ratio = Net Debt / LTM Adj. EBITDA. FCF = Cash flow from Operations less CAPEX (Investing activities), M&amp;A (Investing activities), and interest and leasing payments (Financing activities). Fuels = diesel + gasoline. R&amp;M is refining
and marketing business, it excludes petrochemicals and agro products. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA</B> totaled US$1,124&nbsp;million
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-10%</FONT> q/q), primarily explained by Brent contraction, which affected prices of local fuels and other refined products, besides value of inventories (but also lower unit costs of oil purchases to third
parties), and to a minor extent, decreased working interest in Aguada del Cha&ntilde;ar shale oil block (100%<FONT STYLE="FONT-FAMILY:SYMBOL">&#174;</FONT>51%) since <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Apr-25.</FONT> These effects were partially offset
by lower lifting costs on the back of reduced exposure to mature fields, and peak winter sales of natural gas. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">The
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-7%</FONT> y/y adj. EBITDA was also mostly due to lower price of fuels, partially offset by shale expansions and even better conventional lifting cost. Also, 2Q24 was affected by severe weather in Patagonia. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Excluding mature fields, 2Q25 proxy adj. EBITDA would have been US$1,250</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;million</B>. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX </B>was US$1,160&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-5%</FONT> q/q&nbsp;&amp; <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-3%</FONT> y/y). 71% of
the CAPEX was unconventional (mostly shale), reinforcing the focus on the development of Vaca Muerta. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Shale oil production </B>averaged 145 kbbl/d <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-1%</FONT> q/q and +28% y/y), representing 59% of our total oil production (1Q25: 55% and 2Q24: 46%). Even after reducing stake in Aguada del Cha&ntilde;ar, the <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">ramp-up</FONT> in La
Angostura Sur I <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(South-Hub)</FONT> led the sequential stable level. Moreover, in line with our 4x4 Plan, recently we executed a binding agreement to <B>acquire 45% working interest of La Escalonada and Rinc&oacute;n
La Ceniza blocks</B> (located in the most promising area in the oil and wet gas windows of North Vaca Muerta) from Total Austral S.A. for US$500&nbsp;million, subject to certain conditions and adjustments. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Oil exports</B> reached 44 kbbl/d, being +20% q/q due to exceptional Escalante heavy oil exports (La Plata refinery under programmed maintenance), while
+43% y/y was also driven by shale oil expansion. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Progress on our main projects: </B></P>
<P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

<P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>1. ANALYSIS OF CONSOLIDATED RESULTS OF 2Q25 </B></P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE CELLSPACING="0" CELLPADDING="0" WIDTH="100%" BORDER="0" STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" ALIGN="center">

in addition to increased exports of oil (especially Escalante) and grain and flour (encouraged by lower export duties). These effects were partially offset by Brent contraction impact in our refined products, particularly local fuels, as well as <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">off-peak</FONT> gasoline demand. </P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

(US$91) million for 1H25 and (US$157) million for 1H24&#8195; </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>OPEX</B> totaled US$1,529&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-12%</FONT>
q/q), mainly derived from savings associated with reduced exposure to mature fields and lower OPEX from the remaining fields. <B>Total Other Costs</B> reached US$1,343&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-3%</FONT> q/q), mostly explained
by lower royalties (oil price contraction and lower conventional production, partially compensated by peak winter sales of gas), as well as decreased depreciation and amortizations in the upstream. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Purchases</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; Stock Variations</B> amounted to US$1,340&nbsp;million (+40% q/q). <B>Purchases</B> increased q/q, driven by peak
seasonal agriproducts and natural gas purchases, partially offset by lower crude oil purchases to third parties (as a result of lower unit cost related to Brent contraction). <B>Stock variations</B> reached a negative US$132&nbsp;million in 2Q25
(compared to a positive US$69&nbsp;million in 1Q25), primarily explained by reduced prices which resulted in a lower replacement cost of our inventories.
</P><DIV STYLE="line-height:8.0pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:2pt;border-bottom:1px solid #000000;width:11%">&nbsp;</DIV>

<TD VALIGN="bottom"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; border-top:1.00px solid #000000">&nbsp;</P></TD>
<TD>&nbsp;</TD></TR>
</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Financial net results</B> recorded a loss of US$287&nbsp;million (vs. a loss of US$256&nbsp;million in 1Q25), mostly
explained by updated liabilities and lower domestic interest income rates. <B>Income tax </B>recorded a charge of US$61&nbsp;million (vs. a charge of US$27&nbsp;million in 1Q25) due to higher future tax payables. Consequently, <B>net result</B>
totaled a gain of US$58&nbsp;million (vs. a loss of US$10&nbsp;million in 1Q25). </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>2. ADJ. EBITDA&nbsp;&amp; CAPEX </B></P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled US$1,895&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-8%</FONT> q/q), mainly driven by lower oil
prices associated with Brent contraction and decreased conventional oil production (due to further divestment of mature fields), partially offset by peak seasonal sales of natural gas (also boosted by shale gas expansion). </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Lifting costs</B> decreased to US$12.3/BOE <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-19%</FONT> q/q), mainly as a result of already divested mature fields.
<B>Conventional </B>was US$26.8/BOE <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-12%</FONT> q/q) and <B>unconventional </B>was US$4.1/BOE <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-22%</FONT> q/q, boosted by productivity in the blocks of La Calera, wet gas window,
and Lindero Atravesado, South Hub). Excluding mature fields, total lifting cost would have been around US$7.5/BOE. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Zooming into the lifting costs in our
<B>shale core hub</B> blocks (at 100% stake), it recorded US$4.9/BOE, a competitive level despite the sequential increase (+7% q/q), mainly due to higher pulling and maintenance costs. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Royalties </B>and other taxes averaged US$6.2/BOE <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-7%</FONT> q/q), mostly due to lower crude oil prices, partially offset
by higher gas prices. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Other costs</B> amounted to US$279&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-51%</FONT> q/q), mainly explained by the <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">one-off</FONT> costs related to mature fields mentioned above, partially offset by the positive results from sale of 49% of Aguada del Cha&ntilde;ar. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA</B> totaled US$771&nbsp;million (+1% q/q). Despite Brent volatility that reduced oil prices, it was mainly boosted by higher seasonal gas sales
and enhanced lifting cost driven by lower exposure to mature fields. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> amounted to US$864&nbsp;million
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-12%</FONT> q/q). Despite having lower working interest in Aguada del Cha&ntilde;ar block in 2Q25, over 95% was allocated to unconventional assets (vs. 93% in 1Q25), mostly destined to drilling and workover
activities. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 5/14 </P>

</DIV></Center>

<IMG SRC="g907911dsp00.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Unconventional horizontal oil wells</B> recorded again strong metrics, particularly <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">tied-in</FONT> activities: (# oil wells) </P> <P STYLE="font-size:12pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt" ALIGN="center">


<IMG SRC="g907911g0807171030452.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Note: (1) 1H25 accumulated 95 operated&nbsp;&amp; 10 <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">non-operated.</FONT> </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of <B>efficiencies within our unconventional operations</B>, we achieved new record highs in line with the targets set for 2025. In this sense, we
averaged 331 meters/day of drilling speed in our <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">core-hub</FONT> blocks (+9% q/q), and 259 <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">stages/set-month</FONT> of fracking speed (+10% q/q). </P>

<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom"><I>%&nbsp;</I></TD></TR>
</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil production</B> averaged 248 kbbl/d <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-8%</FONT> q/q), mainly due to the lower
conventional output <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-16%</FONT> q/q), as we progressed with the closing of mature field transactions, and slightly lower shale oil production <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-1.5%</FONT> q/q) due to reduced
working interest in Aguada del Cha&ntilde;ar block. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Natural gas production </B>grew by +6% q/q, mainly explained by the winter peak season (boosted by
increased shale gas output of Rinc&oacute;n del Mangrullo and Aguada de la Arena blocks). <B>NGLs production</B> grew +1.5% q/q, backed on increased associated gas output from certain shale oil fields. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 6/14 </P>

</DIV></Center>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Stock variations include price effects by US$6&nbsp;million in 2Q24, (US$1) million for 1Q25, (US$83) million for 2Q25,
(US$91) million for 1H25 and (US$127) million for 1H24.&#8195; </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Revenues</B> totaled US$3.7&nbsp;billion <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-5%</FONT>
q/q), mainly explained by Brent drop impact on refined products, particularly local fuel prices, partially offset by seasonal sales of agricultural products (exports of grain and flours also boosted by lower export duties) and local diesel demand,
and to a minor extent, increased oil exports. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>OPEX Downstream</B> amounted to US$527&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-1%</FONT> q/q),
mainly due to lower fuel transportation costs, partially offset by higher costs related to refinery maintenances in 2Q25. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Fuel imports</B> reached
US$53&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-10%</FONT> q/q), mainly explained by lower unit cost related to a downward trend in international reference prices. Therefore, total fuel imports remained at low levels, representing only 3% of
total fuel sales, compared to 2% in 1Q25 and 0% in 2Q24. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil purchases </B>(intersegment + third parties) were US$1,765&nbsp;million <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-17%</FONT> q/q), mostly due to lower oil prices, in line with Brent volatility, and lower processing levels due to the stoppage at La Plata Refinery in May. <B>Biofuel purchases </B>increased 8% q/q, mainly due to a 22%
growth in biodiesel (+19% in volume and +2% in price), mainly to supply diesel seasonal demand, slightly offset by 5% decrease in bioethanol <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-4%</FONT> in volume and <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-1%</FONT> in
price), in line with gasoline demand. <B>Agro products purchases </B>grew by 88% q/q, primarily in line with higher seasonal demand of grains and fertilizers. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Stock variations</B> totaled a negative US$114&nbsp;million (vs. a positive US$104&nbsp;million in 1Q25), primarily driven by consumption of inventory in
2Q25 to meet the demand during the programmed maintenance in May. This effect was also affected by lower oil prices in 2Q25. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Other costs </B>decreased
by 10% q/q, primarily driven by the revaluation of Loma Campana &#150; Lago Pellegrini pipeline, slightly offset by higher Midstream Oil costs associated with the new additional capacity at Oldelval. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA,</B> excluding inventories price effect of oil products, totaled US$439&nbsp;million <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-13%</FONT> q/q), mainly
due to lower international prices, which affected our local fuels and other refined products, as well as gasoline demand <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">off-peak</FONT> season. These effects were partially offset by lower cost of oil purchases,
peak local diesel seasonal demand and increased oil exports. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Adj. EBITDA of the Refining</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; Marketing</B> business, in unit
terms, amounted to US$11.9/bbl <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-17%</FONT> q/q), primarily explained by local fuel price contraction (downward trend in international prices), coupled with higher costs related to programmed maintenances in
refineries, slightly offset by lower cost of oil and OPEX efficiency. </P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 7/14 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">

 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX</B> was US$246&nbsp;million (+20% q/q): 57% refining, 25% midstream (O&amp;G), 14% logistics and 4%
others. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In our <B>refineries,</B> CAPEX was allocated for the following main projects: </P>
<P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>New fuel specifications project</B>, to comply with the Resolution No.&nbsp;492/2023 of the Secretary of
Energy. In that sense, the construction of a new diesel oil hydrotreatment unit at Luj&aacute;n de Cuyo refinery continued making progress, expecting to be operational in 1H26. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>
<P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

reconditioning of the refinery to process Vaca Muerta&#146;s shale oil, expected to be in place in 3Q25. </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In our <B>midstream oil</B>
business unit, we continued moving forward with our main projects: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>

a syndicated loan for US$2&nbsp;billion at SOFR+5.5% in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Jul-25</FONT> to finance ~70% of the CAPEX, reopening Project Finance in Argentina. Construction progress: 23% <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(Jul-25),</FONT>

<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom" ALIGN="right">3.5</TD>
<TD NOWRAP VALIGN="bottom">%&nbsp;</TD></TR>
</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Net Average domestic prices for gasoline and diesel are net of taxes, commissions, commercial bonuses and freights.&#8195;
</P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman" ALIGN="right">Page 8/14 </P>

</DIV></Center>


<p style="margin-top:1em; margin-bottom:0em; page-break-before:always"> </p>
<HR SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">


<Center><DIV STYLE="width:8.5in" align="left">

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">


<IMG SRC="g907911dsp00.jpg" ALT="LOGO">
 </P> <p STYLE="margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt ; font-size:8pt">&nbsp;</P>
 <P STYLE="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Crude oil processed </B>averaged 301 kbbl/d <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">(-5%</FONT> q/q), reaching
almost 90% of refinery utilization, mainly due to limited availability at La Plata Refinery (affected by a programmed maintenance in May). </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Domestic
fuels sales volumes</B> reached 3,532 km3 (+4% q/q), on the back of higher diesel seasonal demand, slightly offset by <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">off-peak</FONT> gasoline demand. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Petrochemicals sales volumes</B> grew by 33% q/q, mainly due to higher foreign methanol demand. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Fertilizers, grain and flours sales volumes</B> increased by 41% q/q<B>,</B> mostly explained by higher seasonal demand, especially of grains, boosted also
by lower export duties. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Net average fuels prices in the domestic market measured in dollar terms </B>decreased by 8% q/q, mainly due to lower
international references, partially offset by price adjustments. As a result, the gap with import parities moved to <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-1%</FONT> (vs. +1% in 1Q25 and <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-5%</FONT> in 2Q24). </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Prices for petrochemical</B><B></B><B>&nbsp;&amp; other refined products</B> contracted by 2% q/q, aligned with a downward trend in international prices of
petrochemical and certain refined products, primarily jet fuel, coal and LPG. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>3.3 LNG&nbsp;&amp; INTEGRATED GAS </B></P>

increased seasonal natural gas sales as producer, partially offset higher natural gas purchases and to a minor extent, a charge to doubtful debts. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>CAPEX </B>totaled US$14&nbsp;million, growing significantly vs. 1Q25, mainly due to investments deployed in Argentina LNG project. </P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Regarding the aforementioned project, we made progress in: </P> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="1%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">&#149;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left" STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt"><B>Argentina LNG</B>: Phase 3 (~12 MTPA) signed in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Jun-25</FONT> Heads Agreement
with ENI, the strategic partner. Also, in <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Aug-25,</FONT> SESA (YPF 25% equity stake), the SPV for Argentina LNG 1, obtained the FID approval for a <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">20-year</FONT> bareboat charter
agreement of the second vessel, MK II (3.5 MTPA, COD expected by 2028), and considering the first vessel (2.45 MTPA, COD expected by 2027), total capacity amounts to 5.95 MTPA. </P></TD></TR></TABLE>

increased gas purchases and a recognition of charge for doubtful debts, partially offset by higher seasonal retail gas demand supplied (higher tariff) by our subsidiary Metrogas. </P>

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">FCF = Cash flow from Operations less capex (Investing activities), M&amp;A (Investing activities), and interest and leasing
payments (Financing activities). </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>Free cash flow</B> reached a negative US$365&nbsp;million, mainly due to: (1)&nbsp;impact of mature fields for <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-US$189&nbsp;million;</FONT> (2) <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-US$101&nbsp;million</FONT> for income tax payment from subsidiaries (mainly from Metrogas&nbsp;&amp; AESA); (3) regular debt services amounted to <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-US$100&nbsp;million.</FONT> Moreover, +US$142&nbsp;million of dividend collection from our affiliates, net of contributions and prepayments to them, more than offset the negative working capital variations and others for <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">-US$81&nbsp;million.</FONT> The latter was mostly due to peak seasonal sales of natural gas, agro products and payroll. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In
terms of liquidity, our <B>cash and short-term investments</B> decreased to US$1,011&nbsp;million by the end of June 2025, a contraction of 18% vs. 1Q25. </P>

<P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">As of June&nbsp;30, 2025, YPF&#146;s <B>consolidated net debt</B> totaled US$8,833&nbsp;million (+US$497&nbsp;million q/q), primarily driven by new local
issuances, growing gross debt to US$9,844&nbsp;million, while cash and short-term investments diminished, as mentioned in the previous section. As anticipated before, our <B>net leverage ratio</B> increased from 1.8x in 1Q25 to 1.9x in 2Q25, due to
the closing of mature field transactions. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">In terms of <B>financing</B>, during 2Q25 we tapped the local capital market, issuing two new bonds: </P>
<P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="4%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="5%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">1)</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman; " ALIGN="left">US$-linked bond for US$204&nbsp;million, with a <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">15-month</FONT> tenor at 3.95%
</P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6pt;margin-top:0pt;margin-bottom:0pt">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:10pt" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR style = "page-break-inside:avoid">
<TD WIDTH="4%">&nbsp;</TD>
<TD WIDTH="5%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left">2)</TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P STYLE=" margin-top:0pt ; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman; " ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">Hard-US$</FONT> for US$140&nbsp;million with a
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">2-year</FONT> tenor at 7% </P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">We also secured around US$190&nbsp;million in other local financing:
~US$150&nbsp;million of trade financing and ~US$40&nbsp;million in promissory notes. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">After 2Q25, we issued in the local market: (i)&nbsp;a <FONT
STYLE="white-space:nowrap">US$-MEP</FONT> bond for US$250&nbsp;million, with a <FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">2-year</FONT> tenor at 7.5%; and (ii)&nbsp;a US$-Cable tranche for US$167&nbsp;million, with a
<FONT STYLE="white-space:nowrap">5-year</FONT> tenor at 8.75%. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Additionally, during July, YPF&#146;s credit rating was upgraded by Moody&#146;s credit
agency, after the upgrade in sovereign ratings: from Caa1 to B2, maintaining stable outlook. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Regarding our <B>maturity profile</B>, for the second half
of 2025, the Company faces US$793&nbsp;million of manageable maturities, mostly local: US$467&nbsp;million of short-term trade facilities with local banks; US$120&nbsp;million of export-backed bond amortizations; US$144&nbsp;million of local bonds
and promissory notes; and US$62&nbsp;million of CAF and other financial facilities. </P>

<P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>6. ABOUT YPF </B></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">YPF is the largest energy company in
Argentina, fully integrated in the oil and gas value chain. Our main businesses are: (i)&nbsp;in the upstream, we produce ~40% and ~30% of the country&#146;s oil and gas, respectively, and we are the largest shale producer in Vaca Muerta, in process
of divestment of conventional mature fields; (ii)&nbsp;in the downstream, we operate 3 refineries (~50% of Argentina&#146;s refining capacity) and lead the local diesel and gasoline sales (market share &gt;55%); and (iii)&nbsp;in gas and power,
Metrogas, our subsidiary, distributes ~25% of the country&#146;s natural gas, while YPF Luz, our affiliate, is the third largest power generation company in Argentina. The Government is the controlling shareholder with a 51% stake, and YPF is listed
in the NYSE and ByMA. </P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman"><B>7. DISCLAIMER </B></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6pt; margin-bottom:0pt; font-size:10pt; font-family:Times New Roman">Additional
information about YPF S.A., a sociedad an&oacute;nima organized under the laws of Argentina (the &#147;Company&#148; or &#147;YPF&#148;) can be found in the &#147;Investors&#148; section on the website at <U>www.ypf.com</U>. </P>
